# Project Work Register

This is Triptych's provider-neutral operational memory. Read it together with
`promised-deliverables.toml` before starting or resuming work, after a context
handoff, and before reporting completion. “Published,” “built,” “committed,”
“pushed,” “review copy,” and “complete” are different states.

Last reconciled: 2026-10-30.

## Missal reader English completion, 2026-10-29

<!-- promised-deliverable: missal-reader-english-completion-2026-10-29 -->

**In progress.** The maintainer requests completion of the missal reader,
English translations of all its texts, and maximum useful parallel agent work.
This extends the existing annual-content promise; it does not replace its
appointment, wording, source, review or annual-render requirements. Work
continues on `feature/missal-reader`, preserving all existing accepted text
and source qualifications. The current recovery and source receipts supersede
older numerical summaries in the roadmap.

On 2026-10-30 the maintainer expressly requested coherent commits, regular
reconciliation with current `main`, and pushes to `main`. Keep the workspace
branch name; integrate current main by ordinary merges and publish the verified
result to `origin/main` without force or history rewriting. The rejected old
remote feature history remains excluded. Publication of a validated checkpoint
does not mark the complete-English promise fulfilled.

At intake, fresh owning-report audits identified Roman 1962 composed English availability
of 1,188/1,448 canonical targets, inherited pre-1955 availability of
1,168/1,391, and postconciliar availability of 116/564. These are represented
full-text targets, distinct from Bible-citation rendering availability and
not whole-Missal completeness denominators. English
Ordinary availability is 191/196 modeled Roman elements, 196/206 pre-1955
elements and 0/61 postconciliar elements; modeled placeholders do not all
represent missing translation bodies.

Independent lanes own the fresh census, source/publication-policy review,
Roman Proper recovery and study translations, postconciliar Proper recovery,
each Ordinary family, and reader verification. Exact identified historical
English and original project study English remain distinct. Original study
translations require independently established distributable source wording;
they confer no official liturgical approval. Protected modern originals or
translations require their own distribution basis. Typed gaps and passing
structural renders do not fulfill the requested complete English text.

The Roman Ordinary source lane now supplies English for every currently
represented text body in both historical frames; the three remaining records
in each are appointment stand-ins. Seven new direct English source passages
also have verified prose delivery through the Source Library. The exact
source manifest and delivery successor are retained under
`workflows/reviews/missal-english-completion-2026-10-29/ordinary/`.
Sixteen additional Roman Proper bodies have separately measured, qualified
publication authorities and independent source, English and installed-guard
checks. Their fourteen study translations and two historical translations
remain distinct; the exact authority and receipts are retained under that
review namespace's `roman-1962/` directory. These are bounded accepted
cohorts, not completion of this promise.

Postconciliar availability includes seventy earlier project-study drafts whose
fidelity and underlying-source review remains explicitly open. Independent
acceptance of the new admissions does not retrospectively review those drafts,
approve liturgical use or establish exact 2008 correspondence.

The scope audit also distinguishes real older Holy Week work from historical
nonapplicability. Pre-1955 has four effectively empty rite contexts (Palm
Sunday, its overlapping standalone blessing, Good Friday and the Easter
Vigil). The Lord's Supper still inherits nineteen later-frame Propers despite
its raw unavailable marker; it needs actual older-recension collation and
assembly qualification. Independent source packages and an explicit older
rite model are in progress. A separate pre-1955 Chrism Mass is explicitly
absent and is not missing English work. Postconciliar Common catalogues,
antiphon-only directions and partial formularies also require source-extent
and target correspondence before any fixed number of missing prayers can be
asserted. Complete English cannot be inferred from represented-body counts.

The first accepted source cohort provides composed English for 1,407/1,448
Roman 1962 Proper targets, 1,373/1,391 inherited pre-1955 targets and 155/574

is available for 35/70 modeled elements. Its remaining records include twelve
structural or appointment placeholders and twenty-three represented text gaps.
These counts include the seventy earlier postconciliar drafts with open review
debt and exclude unmodeled whole-book or rite requirements.

## Missal version names and reader inclusion, 2026-10-30

<!-- promised-deliverable: missal-version-nomenclature-2026-10-30 -->

**In progress.** The maintainer requests source-supported names for every
historical point represented by a missal, a firm versioning nomenclature across
the project, retirement of the vague current label “Pre-1955,” and reader
options only for missals with actual contributed content. A dedicated source
and implementation lane is establishing edition, reform horizon, language and
witness distinctions before applying the names consistently. A structural
placeholder alone does not establish contributed text; actual Latin or English
content does. Existing source and completeness qualifications remain binding.

The naming matrix, primary-source citations, consumer inventory and inclusion
rules remain pending. Historical audit bytes and quoted historical labels
remain evidence of their original state; stable-ID migrations need explicit
compatibility handling rather than rewriting accepted source receipts.

## Encyclical completion and English coverage, 2026-10-29

<!-- promised-deliverable: encyclicals-completion-2026-10-29 -->

**In progress.** The maintainer resumed the encyclical work, requested English
translations of all catalogued acts, a classic TeX appearance for the two-column
PDFs, maximum useful parallel work and independent cold review. Existing human
English sources take priority. This pass has introduced no AI-translated
encyclical bodies; catalogue descriptions remain separately identified AI work.

The catalogue checkpoint contains 322 distinct Vatican-indexed acts and 304
external English links: 283 acts have a complete English route, 14 have only
partial or damaged English, and 25 have no located English route. All 91 acts
after 1930 have complete external English routes. These are bounded identity
and extent findings, not claims of translation accuracy or local republication
permission. Thirty-nine complete-English gaps remain open. The local release
contains 4,808 readers, including complete English for 41 acts. The two classic-reader checkpoints now cover
167 of the 327 encyclical source versions; 160 versions still need final
acceptance or source repairs.

All 322 descriptions are present. Their attribution records Claude's 255 and
Codex's 67 contributions; the new descriptions use 66 official English texts
and the explicitly identified Italian Orientales Ecclesias text. The browser
separates complete English, local PDFs, external links and remaining gaps. Its
full method is available in a keyboard-accessible disclosure.

Catalogue validation passes 20 tests including two Chromium checks, all 149
public-alpha test identities, the full source gate, exact release bindings,
site build and independent GitHub Pages artifact verification. Actual built
routes pass six viewport and text-reflow cases, filters and keyboard disclosure
checks. Thirty-one comparison projections change only the source-library tool
hash; comparison content and source evidence are identical. The source-family
bootstrap checkpoint is current while all 164 owner review units remain
honestly pending; no completed family screening is claimed.

Two classic-reader checkpoints now replace 167 of the 327 linked source
versions. The first installed nine cold-reviewed source repairs; the second
adds 158 accepted versions with 917 reviewed pages. Latin Modern Roman,
language-specific hyphenation, indented paragraphs and restrained terminal
apparatus implement the requested classic appearance. Explicit note and native
whitespace transformations preserve source wording and record their actual
scope; independent source-boundary and every-page findings bind exact PDFs.

The release still has 4,808 PDFs and now 9,087 pages. In the second checkpoint,
all 4,650 other complete release rows and PDF bytes are unchanged, including
the first nine replacements. There are no added or withdrawn document keys,
renamed PDF paths or changes to the 304 external English routes. All 6,809
current source selections are accounted for by the complete plan and install
checks. Frozen historical receipts and audits remain scoped to the selected
readers; unrelated historical review files do not authorize new admissions.

The checkpoint passes the complete source and install/fidelity gates, 243
papal tests, 20 catalogue tests, 151 public-alpha tests, exact release bindings,
site build, independent deployment-artifact verification and built-route
viewport, keyboard and no-JavaScript checks. The browser captures match the
previous accepted catalogue. Source-family owner units remain honestly pending.
Current replay helpers are reused by tracked path and exact implementation
hash; older implementations and actual code licenses remain identifiable.

Historical English intake, source-preserving note and spacing repairs, classic
reader installation and the remaining cold-review dispositions continue. A
successful render or producer review does not establish independent page
acceptance. The English coverage, classic-reader and overall completion
requirements below remain open. On 2026-10-30 the maintainer requested regular
coherent commits reconciled and pushed to main; this workspace retains
feature/encyclicals locally and publishes validated checkpoints to origin/main.

## Claude Four Last Things correction, 2026-10-27

<!-- promised-deliverable: claude-flt-correction-2026-10-27 -->

**Complete: corrected, pushed to `main` and verified live.** On 2026-10-27 the maintainer asked
that the Claude work of 26 October be checked for damage from a model
substitution: partway through that work the host switched the coordinating
session and some of its workers from the Claude model to kimi-k3. The switch
was announced inside those sessions but was not acted on, and the records
credited that work to the Claude model. The maintainer declined to add the
substituted model to the contribution records. The correction therefore had
every review that model performed done again by fresh Claude workers, had its
one print-only adjustment re-derived independently, rewrote the records it had
written, and superseded its mechanical steps with Claude rebuilds and
refreshes. The contribution metadata lists only Claude work by exact model
identifier, and kimi-k3 is named only in the correction notes.

The audit found the essay texts sound and the records wrong. In Judgment the
substituted model had given the round-1 verdict on the cold-review repairs,
made both visual reviews and the print-only space reservation, and drafted the
series record and the contribution line. In Death it had written the round-1
visual verdict after the Claude model inspected the pages. Done again by
Claude: the Judgment round-1 recheck, against the sources, found one blocking
defect the earlier verdict had passed (a repair sentence calling the Lyons
profession of 1274 a definition); a two-phrase repair passed at round 2; a
fresh every-page visual review restored the same space reservation, and a
further every-page review passed the repaired build; and every page of the
published Death PDF was reviewed again and found clean. The series record was
rewritten, the Judgment and Death records were corrected, and all four
`generation-metadata.tex` files now list only Claude contributions. The
accounts are the series record's "Correction of 27 October 2026" and the
Judgment record's "Correction, recheck and repair, 27 October 2026".

On 2026-10-28 a further independent Claude audit of the correction commit
`b1dd8c785` rebuilt the four PDFs and converted the four web editions again,
byte for byte identical to the installed files and their recorded hashes, and
confirmed from the session logs that every review the substituted model made
had been done again by Claude. It found record statements that overstated or
misdated the correction: this entry and the series record had called the
switch unannounced and had credited the redo plan to the maintainer; the
series record's list of the substituted model's steps was incomplete; and
smaller figures and section numbers in the Judgment and Death records were
wrong. Those records were corrected on 2026-10-28 with no change to any render
source, PDF or web edition.

The review of the regenerated Judgment web edition also found that it had no
title-page epigraph. No web edition in the series had carried one since
16 October, because the shared title-page macro was set only in print. The
macro now sets the epigraph in the web edition too. A review of all four web
editions then found that four Latin lemmas in Heaven had also been set
without italics on the web since 16 October, because the source nested
`\latin` inside `\emph`; they now use `\latin` alone. The print pages are
unchanged: apart from the revision line, every page of each rebuilt PDF
matches its predecessor. All four essays carry the revision timestamp
2026-10-27T16:55:31Z, and all four web editions were converted again,
reviewed against their PDFs and installed. The account is the series
record's "Web edition repairs, 27 October 2026".

| Essay | Pages | PDF SHA-256 | Web edition SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| I Death | 23 | `321c4346c435299ab6376ce68c1a91fa34face004d903fdcc485c2b6c00d3b3c` | `4a3e7cd75a9c61081a15eb3505f268d2a655b4bf56879a98aa9cef107496a617` |
| II Judgment | 25 | `6ec2bf6a465cecec3800baa2ac2ef59d6b616383d94952e551b875cae4f36555` | `fedbaef01297c60c2cab44d57037476ab1c0904221e31ee4cf2b4dec6865d04a` |
| III Hell | 30 | `3bcfa196950617c075073adc31e904c9298047ca48b1979eed34d3cccd9960f5` | `53596d14d153c2bf0043758e19f1fe45ce4c1b5abe2fb0a5e26dc94dbe9dc38b` |
| IV Heaven | 22 | `53f57926044cfefef5f41dc0de2f1e858d28ae14545f9070c65a2a2e25f6f252` | `185560890437b62851f7aef26bcb39dd594370f936e9c4d5001de398c40632f0` |

The Claude publication inventory, document catalogue and scoped release
bindings are refreshed. The correction published with the All Saints revision:
`origin/main` fast-forwarded to `ffadf81e4e` on 2026-10-28 after the three
deploy gates passed locally, Pages run 40753942788 and repository hygiene run
40753942791 succeeded, and the four live PDFs and web editions matched the
local site build byte for byte, the PDFs also matching the table above, as the

evidence](workflows/reviews/claude-1962-f02-revision-2026-10-27/deployment-evidence.json)
records.

## Claude 1962 All Saints revision, 2026-10-27

<!-- promised-deliverable: claude-1962-f02-revision-2026-10-27 -->

**Complete: accepted, pushed to `main` and verified live.** On 2026-10-27 the maintainer
asked that the Claude work of 26–27 October be checked, because partway
through it the host had switched some stages from the Claude model to kimi-k3;
the switch was announced inside those sessions but was not acted on. For All Saints the audit found that the
substituted model had made the study's last two revisions and their
reviews, the concise study, the homily and every stage after them, and part
of the research. The maintainer decided to repair the publication on the
Claude model before Sunday 1 November 2026, without adding that model as a
provider anywhere:

- the expansive study goes back to its text after author-study iteration 1
  of run `427e2f58bb897a16`, the last text the Claude model wrote, and every
  later revision is made again;
- the research is rebuilt from the sources, with nothing taken over;
- the concise study and the homily are derived again from the revised
  study, and every review is fresh;
- the first run's cycles record gets a short correction, and the leaf's
  contribution metadata lists only Claude work by exact model identifier.

The opening commit restores the study. Its section files match byte for
byte what study-review iteration 0 read, and `generation-metadata.tex` lists
the two author-study contributions as `claude-opus-5-5`. It removes the
earlier research and `propers/` records, the concise study, the homily, the
standing-findings record and the two Patrologia Latina volume 35 artifacts
registered for this production. The first run's archive stays in the leaf
as the record of that run and is not evidence. A new `proper-study` v15 run
revises the leaf in place. It is seeded with the
[research handoff](workflows/reviews/claude-1962-f02-revision-2026-10-27/HANDOFF.md),
which lists the twelve findings of study-review iteration 0, the points
where the later revisions went wrong and the research leads, and keeps the
GPT leaf closed.  Run `450355254538c1ba` reached ACCEPTED on 2026-10-28 with
no escalation: 52 packets, 52 accepted results, one intervention, seven repair
cycles (three in research, one each in the study, the concise study, the
homily and the concise study's layout). Every accepted agent stage ran on
`claude-opus-5-5` at its packet's effort. Through author-study 0 the workers
were Factory Droid sessions with the model pinned, each accepted only after
its session log showed the Claude model alone; one research attempt the host
switched to kimi-k3 was rejected and its files removed, and one study-review
result whose session closed on kimi-k3 was set aside and the review done
again. From study-review 0 the workers were fresh Claude Code harness
subagents. The installed PDFs are the expansive study (31 pages,
`08f8c5e99572d5f8a9584555c47b901bc32abd09fdf74dd5d7094f387143c3bc`), the
concise study (11 pages,
`fb899d0258fe16641fb2be454eb088f37fd41011012fe599b5fb2d5ac397c881`) and the
homily (4 pages,
`a6630b389583e729bbaa18103fb48364e2767d47485ecbddeb4ddbef4d3bc2ac`); the web
edition is `cbe405ad17c9ba6be5d64d37891a282e99700df9a1ded3528ab595931cf0438c`.
The run is archived in the leaf; its [cycles
record](workflows/reviews/claude-1962-f02-revision-2026-10-27/CYCLES.md) says
what became of every handoff finding and lead and lists the advisories that
stand, among them the physician answer at Aquinas, *In Ps.* 33 n. 10, which
the research and both studies credit to Aquinas though he reports it as
Augustine's (the homily is right). The archive tool's privacy audit was fixed
after acceptance (`b38044fe8`) so that the run could be archived. The Claude
publication inventory and the source-family ledger are refreshed. branch twice
(`2d87e1cad8`, `ffadf81e4e`), each time re-recording only this branch's
site-source bindings; after the three deploy gates passed locally,
`origin/main` fast-forwarded to `ffadf81e4e` on 2026-10-28. Pages run
40753942788 and repository hygiene run 40753942791 succeeded, and the three
live PDFs and the web edition matched the local site build and the reviewed
bytes, as the [deployment
evidence](workflows/reviews/claude-1962-f02-revision-2026-10-27/deployment-evidence.json)
records.

## Claude 1962 All Saints, 2026-11-01

<!-- promised-deliverable: claude-1962-f02-all-saints-three-documents-2026-11-01 -->

**Complete: accepted, pushed to `main` and verified live.** On
2026-10-26 the maintainer asked for the Claude 1962 proper-study documents
for the upcoming Sunday and authorized publication to `main`. Sunday,
1 November 2026 is All Saints (I class), permanent identity `F02`,
`liturgy/roman-rite/1962/propers/temporal/f02-all-saints`; the Twenty-third
Sunday after Pentecost is commemorated. The production plan records the
one-provider authorization. This is an independent Claude production: the
published GPT leaf for the same identity is not an input, and no stage
opened any GPT-provider file or record for the identity.

Work began at the authorization commit `c7dfb84c7` on
`feature/propers/claude`; publication is an ordinary fast-forward of
`origin/main` after the three deploy gates pass locally. Run
`427e2f58bb897a16` (`proper-study` v15) reached ACCEPTED at its
publication gates with no escalation: 45 packets, 45 accepted results, one
recorded intervention. Every agent stage ran as a fresh Factory Droid
worker subagent with no inherited conversation, and every review was a
fresh worker. Six cycles were real defects
that program gates or cold reviews found and repaired: two in research (a
misreported 1570 Offertory, a wrong Offertory collation and psalm
numbering, a Missal reported read but unread; then unsealed Lasance English
and unregistered dated web witnesses), one house-voice preflight failure,
one round where a research false negative hid Victorinus of Pettau on
Apoc 7 and the study carried six defects of its own, one carry-through gap
where that research repair never reached the study prose, and one homily
note that misrouted a Gal 3:29 quotation. The Latin rests on the 1962
Missal read on the RGMR facsimile page images, with the 1570, 1604, 1862
and 1889 layers collated; the Preface is deliberately left unsettled, both
candidates named and neither asserted. The installed family is a 32-page
expansive study, a 12-page concise study, a four-page homily (about 1,445
spoken words) and the canonical web edition. The run is archived in the
leaf under `evaluations/proper-study-results/427e2f58bb897a16/`. The
driver's account covers every cycle, the five standing advisories and what
is left for other owners:
[CYCLES.md](workflows/reviews/claude-1962-f02-production-2026-10-27/CYCLES.md).
The three local deploy gates passed, and `origin/main` was fast-forwarded
from `59a9862cc` to `6f93e73e9`. Pages run 40501677604 succeeded. The
three PDFs, the web edition, the Traditional Latin Mass catalog page, the
browser's corpus data and the publication manifest are each byte-identical
live to the verified local site, as
[deployment-evidence.json](workflows/reviews/claude-1962-f02-production-2026-10-27/deployment-evidence.json)
records.

Corrected 2026-10-27 (see "Claude 1962 All Saints revision, 2026-10-27"
above): this entry first said that every stage ran at its packet's declared
effort. The Claude workers ran at `high`, the reviews included, and part of
the production ran on a substituted model, kimi-k3; the cycles record's
correction says which stages. The revision supersedes this production.

### Verified register publication, 2026-10-27

`origin/main` had advanced to `da270a039` during the work, so it was
merged without rewriting either parent; the only conflict,
`release/public-alpha.json`, took the upstream manifest and re-recorded only
the catalogue and the four Claude web editions. The deploy gates
(`make check-deployment-sources`, `make public-site`,
`public-alpha verify --deployment-target github-pages`) and
`make check-sources` passed on the merged tree. Commit
`ff484b0e3163f4e64c4a48aa14628d0e3ef20f8d` reached `origin/main` by
ordinary fast-forward. [Pages run 40422812019](https://github.com/spincyc/triptych/actions/runs/40422812019)
and [repository hygiene run 40422812013](https://github.com/spincyc/triptych/actions/runs/40422812013)

**In progress.** Source and organize papal encyclicals, bulls, and related

The first acquisition boundary is the Vatican's mixed historical collections
and modern document-category indexes, supplemented by the printed Bullarium,
ASS/AAS volume inventories, and identified historical English editions.
An index's coverage is recorded independently from the universe of historical
papal acts. Historical volume acquisition does not count as individual-document
segmentation. Modern translation rights, missing original-language witnesses,
unresolved dates, index errors, and unsegmented volumes remain explicit gaps.

The canonical ownership remains the provider-neutral source library. Exact
source bytes, original works, translations, and generated source editions stay
distinct. Publicly retained text needs its own affirmative rights basis;
public accessibility and an act's date alone do not establish it. Full-text
retrieval, normalized text, typesetting, visual review, installation, and public
distribution are separately recorded states. The owning collection records are
under `src/sources/papal-documents/`; the profile is
`guidance/papal-documents.md`.

On 2026-10-26 the maintainer confirmed both coverage layers: English and Latin
where available, the linked Vatican formal-document collections, and

boundaries, with explicit holds for interleaved columns, displaced notes,
uncertain issuers, duplicate scans and unavailable material. Later protected
bodies remain metadata-only. Printed attribution does not authenticate an early
or disputed act.

The Vatican index snapshot contains 2,814 source-index records across 237

within one controlling delivery.

date and AAS volume label do not establish publication before 1931. A

readers are withheld while its factual metadata and evidence remain. Earlier

**Integrated and published; maintainer terminal acceptance remains open.**
The upstream work records a maintainer request for a comprehensive, elegant,
intuitive and beautiful btop-like terminal reader, starting with the Missal and
extended to Catena Omnia, with clarification, a sanity check and a cold review.
Decisions taken with the maintainer on 22 October 2026: Python and Textual;
read the local checkout rather than the published site; after Catena, cover

request to push to `main`); collapse Catena's author groups by default. This
continuation incorporates that result, not its publication authority.

The official correction's printed abbreviation error is recorded explicitly;

## Missal reader C1 truthful coverage, 2026-10-19

<!-- promised-deliverable: missal-reader-c1-coverage-2026-10-19 -->

The maintainer requested completion of the first implementation phase of the
[daily reader completion plan](guidance/liturgy-browser-roadmap.md#completion-plan-2026-10-19),
with regular validated commits and publication to `feature/missal-reader`.
The target is C1: an expected-target inventory grounded in identified books,
one typed coverage report, and truthful language/witness controls and notices.
C2–C6, corpus text acquisition, and production deployment remain separate
milestones. Existing ritual-flow and historical acceptance records retain
their scope.

**Complete on `feature/missal-reader`; no production deployment.** Every C1
requirement passes. Completion was published as `ec0816c59`, and the remote
`feature/missal-reader` ref was verified to match it. The maintainer made three decisions on 2026-10-19, recorded
in the roadmap's C1 status:

- **1920 witness.** The circulating "1920 typical edition" files are public
  domain for their 1920 content and usable. They are the registered Bonn 2004
  *reimpressio* of a conformed edition of about 1954, accepted as the pre-1955
  witness with the 1922 Mame printing as control. Text first published in 1931
  or later stays outside that rights decision.
- **2008.** Acquiring the complete 2008 book is deferred.
- **Performance.** Load-time work is deferred to C6.

Four parallel lanes then enumerated the whole Bonn witness: 45 slices and 8,274
targets, every artifact page owned by a section slice or an extent boundary.
322 targets sit in post-1922-additions slices, each absence confirmed on 1922
Mame page images. The two older pre-1955 Holy Week slices, 42 rows each mapped
one-to-one to a new row, are superseded. After current-main integration and the
bounded postconciliar Pentecost accounting, the inventory holds 18,947 targets
in 118 slices with 108 boundaries. All three whole-book universes stay
`unresolved`: 1962's text slots and other dimensions, pre-1955's
not-yet-reviewed whole-book dimensions, and the deferred 2008 book. No
denominator is asserted beyond what the slices state.

The C2 audit later found that coverage never received the Latin provenance
relation, so every report said "0 exact-target". C2 step 1 fixed it: roman-1962
now reports 1,377 exact-target Latin uses (1,068 targets).

The retry's real-browser checks pass all 91 assertion groups: forty Propers,
eight choices and 43 Day, against the corrected production state. Independent print review
found that selecting a partial Caswall witness falsely said no English or Latin
body was held where Cummiskey English was available. The shared renderer now
preserves `absent-in-witness` before the broad missing-body fallback; actual
All Souls unit and live-browser regressions pass. Harness fixes retain the
failure-race assertions while correcting their source-only URLs, wait for
profile cleanup, permit an isolated scratch location, and exclude scratch
artifacts from the publication-route scan. Source and final visual acceptance
remain open; these are local implementation checkpoints, not C1 completion.
The focused Python run exercised 139 tests with two opt-in browser skips;
its sole initial error was the stale shared-renderer release binding. That
binding was refreshed and its exact guard rerun passes.

A second retry on 19 October recovered the interrupted session's scratch
research and ran four fresh parallel lanes: 1962 inventory, postconciliar
2008/U.S. 2011, browser acceptance, and 1920/2008 acquisition.

- **1962.** The interrupted Lent enumeration was verified against OCR role
  headings, marginal numbers and 45 page images. Every section of the
  registered facsimile was then consolidated: 35 slices, 7,621 targets and
  30 boundaries. Every artifact page 1–1088 is owned by a section slice or an
  explicit extent boundary, and five duplicates of existing identities were not
  added.
- **Whole-book boundaries.** An independent review compared the 1962
  targets with self-derived date, rank and Introit text checks, the book's
  index, and 238 page images. It found no missing or phantom formulary heading
  and exactly sixteen Prefaces. Nine kind inconsistencies were corrected and two
  OCR-missed Preface appointments added. The form and Preface boundaries are
  retired: 601 Mass formularies under one stated rule. The slice stays
  unresolved for text slots and the other dimensions.
- **2008 Latin.** Twenty existing 2002 appointments are joined to the official
  2008 variation list. This establishes identity only and makes no
  unchanged-wording claim.
- **U.S. 2011.** A 210-heading General Index slice was added from the newly
  registered Catholic Book Publishing preview. The preview is restricted;
  metadata only is kept.
- **Totals.** The inventory now holds 10,683 targets in 72 slices with 91
  boundaries. None of the three whole-book universes is resolved.
- **Browser acceptance.** Real Chromium checked ten Day/Propers states at
  1024×768 and 768×1024, at 200% and 400% zoom equivalents, with text-only 200%
  enlargement, forced colors, reduced motion, touch, an accessibility-tree
  proxy, and real Tab focus containment.
  - Fixed: the Browse formulary list labelled coverage in the wrong language
    (introduced by C1); the forced-colors Apply label was invisible; an English
    composed-text label was tagged Latin; and a zero denominator read "0 of 0".
    Each has a harness or unit regression.
  - Recorded for C4 and ritual-flow: the 400%-zoom Contents jump under the
    action bar, 12 px notices, closed-select truncation, untagged Latin
    headings, the pre-1955 "Selection unavailable" state without a formulary,
    and postconciliar "No English or Latin body" wording beside restricted
    units.
- **Layout shift.** Throttled-mobile cold CLS fell from 0.117–0.203 to under
  0.01. The cause was the empty reading box, which moved as the identity block
  and the longer notice arrived. It is now hidden while only its loading line
  is present. Settled and loading pixels are unchanged; the reader still sees
  the loading line replaced by content. While loading, the `main` landmark and
  skip-link target are therefore not exposed.
- **Payload.** The published structures no longer carry per-target inventory
  rows, which the browser never read. They keep exact state totals and the one
  unrepresented target. Constant per-Mass report fields are also dropped, and
  the three calendar files are written compactly, one mass per line. Gzip sizes
  are now: postconciliar 342,797, roman-1962 1,171,066 and pre-1955 441,251
  bytes. Before C1 they were 309,406, 1,086,641 and 378,778.
- **Load time.** Day 1962 cold LCP on throttled mobile is 12.21 s, against
  11.89 s before C1 and 14.54 s at its peak. Postconciliar is 8.21 s against
  8.13 s. Both still fail the vision's 2.5 s budget, as before C1; warm and
  desktop loads pass. Larger reductions, a lazily fetched coverage sidecar or
  per-Mass files, need reader changes and are recorded in the payload lane's
  proposals.

Work began from verified feature-branch baseline
`8f7be05286739235d590833eddba538e7e069ce0`; the implementation contract was
published as `13e7a190ee4a9771810bf9e516c5490a32b4792b`.
The first [source inventory](src/sources/inventories/missal-expected-targets-v1.toml)
checkpoint, `1f8cf65bb1d692b08115665eb8ab74258567f99c`, published 938
independently grounded identities. The common-report and reader implementation
was published as `cd6ce81a526238728e3d27230b5c46524c1052a8`. The source
checkpoint `2d698a79b26f0df49fd6e71c4ade016a4b224733`
contains 2,850 identities in 36 bounded slices and 57 explicit
boundaries, including sixty appointments/referrals directly established from
the official 2008 supplement. These heterogeneous identities are not a
whole-book text-slot denominator. Its
[source review](src/sources/inventories/missal-expected-targets-review-2026-10-19.md)
distinguishes source-witness enumeration from target-edition correspondence.
The 1962 calendar identifies Saint Anastasia's Christmas commemoration even
though no represented Mass row exists. None of the three whole-book universes
is fully enumerated. The accessible 2002 book's named structural queues have
been inventoried at their declared granularity; correspondence with the exact
2008 target remains unresolved unless directly evidenced.

Completion now requires an authenticated 1920 Vatican typical witness,
readable full evidence for the registered 1962 witness, and an authenticated
complete 2008 body/index or documented correspondence for each bounded slice,
together with the selected US 2011 edition's structural dispositions. The
source review records the attempted URLs and retrieval limits. A 1922
conformed printing, a 2002 antecedent, and partial later transcriptions cannot
establish those target denominators.

The shared report now drives generated Proper/Ordinary structures, the CLI
audit, and Day/Propers controls, discovery and selected-Mass notices. It separates
source-owned targets, appointed uses and published witness alternatives;
preserves mixed composed/Scripture components; and exposes partial witnesses
without substituting another translation. Publication-basis metadata does not
grant authorization, and protected artifact aliases are excluded.

Independent implementation review reproduced and verified fixes for alias
conflicts, mixed-only witnesses, nested translation-only cycles, form-scoped
target joins, and public wording beside restricted alternatives. The unchanged
publication scanner accepts all six generated structures and still rejects
protected identities and wording inserted into coverage metadata. The combined
regression suite passes 279 tests with six existing skips; nineteen captured
examples replay without divergence. Source checks, release bindings, and tool
registry checks pass. The full-year gate passes all 4,380 live JSON/text
renderings across 2,190 date/calendar/language cases. Final source-snapshot
coverage tests pass (53 tests). Updated browser fixtures use real partial
witnesses and regenerate their coverage through the shared derivation; their
static/harness regression runs pass 126 tests with two opt-in browser skips,
plus 21 Day integration tests. The last fixture assertion was rechecked by
the offline fixture check and seven harness tests (two browser skips).

The initial run could not perform real-browser verification because the
environment refused local listener and Chromium sandbox system calls. Those
restrictions are now removed and verification is running; no responsive or print
acceptance is inferred from the passing unit tests. With the locked Markdown 3.10.3
dependency restored under scratch from verified upstream blobs, 130 of 131
broader public-alpha tests pass. Its sole error is the unchanged absence of
`pdf/gpt/theology/mariology/ark-of-the-covenant.pdf` in this source-only checkout;
no publication-filter code was changed. The promise-ledger
check retains exactly its 32 baseline missing-artifact findings.
Proper payloads increase by approximately 0.16–0.43 MB compressed per family;
live load/parse impact is being measured in the browser retry.
The first four feature-branch checkpoints used the connected GitHub API with
an expected-head lease and matching local trees while shell networking was
unavailable. A normal Git fetch now succeeds. The last checkpoint includes the
bounded inventory, corrected browser fixtures, generated data/bindings, and the
initial blocked disposition.
That disposition describes the first run; the retry is active and does not
authorize production deployment or close C1.

Completion requires independently reviewable target and occurrence identities,
separate witness counts, distinct availability/rights/wording relationships,
browser/generated-data/CLI parity, regression and browser evidence, and
verified feature-branch publication. Known missing texts may remain typed
gaps; neither a valid report nor a full inventory asserts textual completeness.

## Missal reader C4 dialogue cues, 2026-10-19

<!-- promised-deliverable: missal-reader-c4-dialogue-cues-2026-10-19 -->

After C1's acceptance checkpoint the maintainer asked for the next parallel
steps. C4's first ordered step is the dialogue cue contract in the
[roadmap](guidance/liturgy-browser-roadmap.md#c4-dialogue-typography-and-usability-contract).

**In progress: steps 1 and 2 implemented.** Step 2 gives Introibo/Judica, the
Preface dialogue, the Pax and the dismissal reviewed per-turn rows, roles from
the 1962 typica's printed marks, and headings without printed cues.

- **Opening cue.** `spoken()` now keeps an all-speaker dialogue's printed
  opening attribution as a language-tagged "Priest" word label, so the
  postconciliar Preface dialogue and Pax Domini no longer lose it. Final word
  labels keep their space in announced and copied text.
- **One visible cue.** Ordinary turns may declare a `printed_marker` (`P.` or
  `R.`). `tools/mass-ordinary` accepts it only for a row that is exactly that
  marker, a space and the spoken words, and refuses an undeclared opening
  marker on a spoken turn. The renderer then shows one cue per verified turn:
  ℣/℟ where a role is declared, otherwise the speaker. This covers the 1962
  Confiteor and Suscipiat, the postconciliar Orate fratres, and the existing
  Laus tibi and Deo gratias turns. Source text is unchanged byte for byte.
- **CSS exception removed.** The Orate-fratres-only rule is gone.
- **Evidence.** Real-browser checks cover visible cue count, accessibility
  names and clipboard text; unit tests cover the generator guards.

Remaining:

- Twenty opaque dialogue blocks still need review and division.
- ℣/℟ typography and turn-row layout belong to later C4 steps.
- The postconciliar dismissal still has no sourced text.

The same request starts C2. **In progress: exemplars chosen; steps 1 (truthful accounting), 3

most days with a typed Preface stand-in. Recording the printed directions and
the rubric spans then brought 1962 to a seated Preface on every day of 2026.
Pre-1955 keeps 206 stand-in days until commemorations and octaves are modelled.
The formulary-level expected-target join (step 2) is done: 488 1962 and 463
pre-1955 formularies are joined. A read-only real-browser audit chose Pentecost Sunday,
2026-05-24, for all three missals and audited every unit against the six-part
pattern. The accepted Bonn pre-1955 witness inventory has since landed;
its Pentecost text slots still need their source joins and wording collation.
The initial audit found 22 gaps, now recorded in the
[roadmap](guidance/liturgy-browser-roadmap.md#c2-exemplars-and-execution-plan-2026-10-19).
The most serious are:

  "0 exact-target" (a C1 accounting defect);
- Missal mode silently seats the common Communicantes and Hanc igitur in
  Pentecost's proper seats, and prints all eleven 1962 Prefaces.

Two open questions were settled from existing guidance: the 2026-09-03
word-agreement rule makes collated word-exact projections `exact-target`, and

The 2026-10-20 continuation joined the 1962 Pentecost exemplar's ten Proper

Fresh source-served Chromium runs passed 55 Day and 49 Propers assertion
groups with no console or request failures. The 1962 exemplar was captured
at 1440, 393 and 320 CSS pixels without horizontal overflow, and its print

Date-free postconciliar Propers intentionally omits two unresolved Ordinary

completed technical checks from the remaining source and product acceptance:

represented with all thirteen unresolved, and postconciliar has no joined

Day and Propers now share validated missal, Bible and oration-language
preferences, written only after successful Date or Browse Apply. URLs retain
precedence, and bad or blocked storage falls back without affecting explicit
state. A dedicated source-served Chromium run passes 11/11; Day and Propers
regressions pass 55/55 and 49/49. This is a bounded C5 capability, with
independent acceptance and the remaining modes still open.
The English witness chooser now identifies itself as a Mass-wide choice
beside the first Proper. Pentecost Chromium checks pass for the chooser and
for Caswall's explicit Introit gap and held Sequence in Day and Propers;
partial-witness deep links remain valid.
An independently page-checked twelve-role Sanctoral batch brings the effective
Roman 1962 text-slot inventory to 3,194 represented, 166 not represented and
2,925 unresolved; all 7,990 target kinds now classify 4,017 / 167 / 3,806.
These Gospel and chant citations remain labelled Bible reconstructions where
Missal wording is unestablished. Eight other proposed chant joins were held
back after printed wording checks; Gabriel's printed Luke locator discrepancy
also remains open. An Adjutorium derivative now divides the printed 1962
priest/answer dialogue into source-bound ℣/℟ turns without changing its words.
A print-only pagination rule keeps a short translation credit beside its
witness account; source-served Day and Propers English Read remain seven pages
with identical extracted text, and checked Latin layouts are unchanged.
St Lucy's Communion now has a hash-bound exact 1962 Latin antiphon collated
with the public-domain 1922 Mame witness; its Psalm citation remains separate.
Roman 1962 exact-target Latin Proper availability rises to 1,074 of 1,203
represented composed targets. A further page-checked St Thomas Apostle
Introit uses the complete 1922 Mame antiphon, Psalm verse and Gloria Patri cue,
collated against the 1962 typica. It raises that bounded count to 1,075 of
1,204; English remains an explicit no-exemplar gap. The Missal prints
`honorati` where the Bible verse has `honorificati`, so the Bible body is not
substituted. Proposed St Anthony Offertory and St Michael Introit Scripture
joins were held for the same wording reason. Neither result resolves the
whole-family text denominator. The CLI now labels Bible reconstructions and
separately shown Bible text. A generated, form-qualified Propers search index
and a pure two-side semantic Compare core are checked data foundations; their
reader controls and independent acceptance remain open.
The subsequent source collation recovers those two held chants from exact
Missal wording: St Anthony's Offertory and St Michael's Introit agree with
page-checked public-domain Mame passages, including their appointed context.
Both retain Bible citations as structural references, exact-target Latin
provenance, and typed English no-exemplar rows. Effective 1962 target states
are now 4,020 represented, 164 not represented and 3,806 unresolved;
source-inventoried joined slots are 2,965 represented and 392 not represented.
The complete-Missal check reports 1,077 exact-target Latin Proper texts out
of 1,206 represented composed targets.
This remains bounded source coverage, not whole-book text completion.

The next C5 integration seats Propers Missal over the same source-backed
Ordinary and Proper events as Day. The 1962 Pentecost fixture yields 179
ordered events: 163 Ordinary events, six divisions and ten Propers. The view
retains explicit alternatives and date-unknown applicability, preserves
ordinary language and option choices through URL and Read/Missal changes, and
keeps source-authored non-Mass frames explicit. Its source-served Chromium
suite passes 56/56 assertions, including a same-document Ordinary-location
navigation regression. Independent source-served Chromium QA passed 9/9
checks at narrow and 400% reflow widths, exercised postconciliar Day/Vigil
and Ordinary choices, and inspected the first page of the 24-page 1962 print.
The built-preview visual harness passes 28/28, the production Propers browser
suite passes 56/56, and the private preview verifies. Full C5 acceptance
remains open.
The next C5 source run makes the form-qualified Propers index searchable from
both entrances, loading it on demand and linking to exact edition/form
results with its coverage boundary visible. Day Compare now resolves each
edition's calendar outcome independently on a fixed date and territory before
pairing functional Proper slots. Propers Compare holds one date-independent
formulary and Bible fixed while adapting two oration languages independently.
Explicit URL state, Contents location, focus return, absent events and
unavailable text have source-served browser checks: 10/10 Day Compare and
63/63 Propers Read/Missal/Search/Compare. The shared view and state contract
pass focused tests. The private preview verifies and all seven registered
built-preview browser harnesses pass; the focused source/UI suite passes 230
tests. Study and independent C5 acceptance remain open.
The Study continuation attaches a contextual apparatus to the same Read DOM
and semantic event IDs in both entrances. Day shows its actual calendar
result, coverage and source/availability facts; Propers states its
date-independent boundary. Source-served Chromium passed 10/10 Day Study and
66/66 Propers Read/Missal/Search/Compare/Study checks, with preserved text,
Contents, location and print. Day `mode=study&ordinary=0` is live; an explicit
`ordinary=1` remains deferred without partial rendering. The shared adjunct
passes five focused tests. All seven registered built-preview browser
harnesses pass after the Study integration. Independent C5 acceptance remains
open.
Independent private-preview QA covered 29 responsive Study/Compare states and
ten Search variants without horizontal overflow or browser errors. At 400%
zoom, the Day Compare Calendar-results Contents jump now lands visibly; Mode
dialogs keep keyboard focus inside and restore it on Escape. The built-preview
visual harness locks those regressions at 30/30. Print keeps short Coverage,
Study-note and unavailable-Compare groups together; inspected PDFs are ten
pages for Day Study, eight for Propers Study and twelve for Day Compare. The
long Day Study print remains C4 composition work, and this review does not
establish full C5 acceptance.

A source-inventoried bounded form report now keeps its parent form identity and
counts the 1962 Pentecost Day appointments at 13 expected and 13 represented.
Missing Lesson or changed Preface negative controls fall to 11/13. A partial
source slice reports only a lower bound. Whole-form extent remains unresolved,
as do all 39 whole-family completeness cells. The printed 1962 closing
`Deo gratias` also now carries the English and Latin response role without
changing the source words.

St Lucy's collated Communion is now explicitly marked as lacking an English
antiphon witness in the translation inventory; inherited pre-1955 coverage
carries the same typed absence. Both English inventories have zero unaccounted
Proper slots. The Latin substitution audit exempts only the source-verified
`gratis` in that Communion, leaving zero page-image candidates; it does not
substitute the differing Bible expansion for the Missal wording.
The C4 cue pass enlarges and colors the printed ℣/℟ turns while preserving
their accessible and copied text, 320-pixel reflow and print. A print rule
keeps a short Collect translation credit beside its witness note. The
source-served Day integration harness passed its new glyph case; the built
preview and independent C4 matrix remain separate checks.

Independent C2 review of the three Pentecost exemplars confirms the event
order and appointed seats. The 1962 example has ten exact-target Latin
Propers, while its English Lesson and two Communicantes passages remain
unwitnessed. Postconciliar Day is accepted only as a partial state: target
Latin and approved English cannot be inferred from the study Collect,
Caswall Sequence or Bible reconstructions. Pre-1955 remains a structural
projection with two typed proper-Canon absences and no collated full 1920
target witness. Event parity therefore closes the ordering check, not
word-exact or rights acceptance.

A read-only C6 lab probe of committed preview `eb8ac428b` measured narrow
cold LCP at 12.5–12.9 seconds for Day and Propers on a Slow-4G/4×-CPU
profile, versus the 2.5-second vision target. Warm narrow loads were below
0.61 seconds; desktop cold loads were 1.56–1.62 seconds. The 1962 Proper
structure contributes about 1.23 MB gzipped and dominates the cold
transfer. These are two-run lab samples, not field percentiles.

The first C6 load-time change is committed and pushed as `f671358ba`.
Eligible resolved Roman 1962 Read links on Day and Propers now fetch a
validated catalog and one of 491 exact Mass envelopes; unresolved choices,
cross-Mass dependencies, other modes and editions, and split-data failures
use the existing complete structure. The generator test reconstructs every
Mass exactly, the public-preview gate checks generation, rights and source
parity for all 491 files, and the built Chromium suite passed seven tests.
The private preview was rebuilt and verified; this is not a production
deployment or C6 release acceptance.

On the same two-run Slow-4G/4×-CPU lab profile, cold mobile LCP became
7.68–7.74 s for canonical Day Read and 7.01–7.02 s for Propers Read. Transfer
fell from 1.49 MB to 319 KB on Day and from 1.43 MB to 269 KB on Propers;
cold desktop LCP was 0.69–0.75 s. The Day comparison removes an explicit
`mass=` parameter because a derived, settled Day choice is the safe split
path; both links resolve to the same `pentecost-10` Mass. These lab samples
remain above the 2.5 s mobile target and do not establish field p75. The
remaining C6 work is page startup and request sequencing, followed by the
complete C5/print/accessibility and independent release review.

The next C5 checkpoint adds shared Mode-radio keyboard behavior and secondary
Share/Print actions on both entrances. The selected radio is the group's single
Tab stop; Arrow, Home/End, Space and Enter use the same controller choices.
Share sends a canonical reader URL and identity title without liturgical text,
with a local-copy fallback. Oration-language comparisons name the peer
language, while recension comparisons name the peer edition. Print uses the
browser dialog and excludes the controls. The keyboard probe passes eighteen
checks across both entrances; the built Chromium gate passes all seven tests.

The second C6 change overlaps safe 1962 Read catalog loading with existing
manifest/rubric requests and fetches the selected Mass alongside that catalog.
Generation, path, identity and fallback validation still govern the result.
On the same two-run Slow-4G/4×-CPU lab profile, cold mobile LCP is 7.34–7.35 s
for Day and 6.36–6.37 s for Propers; cold desktop samples are 0.60–0.70 s.
The added controls bring transfer to about 325 KB and 274 KB. These samples
remain above the 2.5 s mobile target and do not establish field p75 or
independent C5/C6 acceptance.

The next C5 usability slice replaces the dock's Details action with direct
Study on both entrances. Details remains in Mode and keeps source context,
Share and Print reachable. Direct Study preserves the held semantic place and
returns focus to its dock command; moving between modal surfaces returns to
the visible Mode command. A native 100–200% text-size control remembers valid
display preferences independently of semantic URL state, works without
storage, preserves place and focus, and leaves print at its own size. Combined
reduced-motion testing caught and fixed stale scroll restoration through
Mode → enlargement → Details → close. The integrated focused probes pass
eighteen keyboard checks, twelve direct-Study checks, three text-size cases
and four Share/Print cases, with clean browser diagnostics.

The print continuation tightens Study fact spacing and gives Compare paired
columns while preserving unavailable-side protection and the final semantic
pair. The four checked PDFs change from 10/12/8/9 pages to 7/9/7/6 for Day
Study, Day Compare, Propers Study and Propers Compare. Extracted liturgical
and source content is preserved; representative long-reading and final-page
rasters were inspected. This bounded print evidence does not close the full
C4 composition matrix or independent C5 acceptance.

The next C5 slice enables Day Study with `ordinary=1` through the existing
Missal renderer. Seven full-order fixtures preserve text, event order,
branches, choices, absences, coverage and semantic place across Missal and
Study; moving to Read maps to the nearest Proper event. The expanded Day
Study Chromium probe passes 25 checks, including missing-resource refusal.

Propers Compare now supports an explicitly chosen held lectionary cycle for
the same formulary. The Transfiguration fixture pairs its recorded A/B/C
Gospels while holding edition, form, Bible and language choices fixed. These
remain Bible reconstructions; neither exact Missal wording nor civil-year
applicability is inferred. Eight contract checks and eight Chromium checks
cover coequal selection, invalid peers, source boundaries and peer-state
cleanup. A print regression also keeps source credits with the preceding
text tail without preventing long readings from paginating. Independent
C5 acceptance and the remaining comparison axes are still open.

The next C6 startup slice moves bounded response hints immediately after the
shared browser core on the four modern reader entrances. Only explicit eligible
1962 Read URLs qualify, using the core's existing data-root policy. Hints warm
responses; the normal generation, identity, rights and fallback checks still
admit them. The unchanged Ordinary presenter now loads only when validated
state requests it, including Day Study with the Ordinary. Concurrent requests
share one load; failure permits Read recovery and a later Missal retry, and an
old response cannot replace a newer selection. Retained eager visual oracles
remain supported. Focused Chromium probes pass nine startup-hint checks and
eleven Ordinary-loading checks with no unexpected diagnostics. A first Missal
visit can pay an additional code request after state validation; this tradeoff
and cold Read timing are measured separately from semantic correctness.

On a frozen C9 baseline versus the combined startup change, two cold HTTP/2
samples per entrance at 562.5 ms latency, 1.47456 Mbps down and 4× CPU give
Day LCP 5.328/5.360 → 4.600/4.668 s and Propers 4.552/4.368 → 4.000/3.880 s.
Transfer falls 330,659 → 303,215 bytes and 280,651 → 253,210 bytes respectively.
The earlier 7.34/6.36 s samples above used HTTP/1.1; transport and the newly
frozen baseline are explicit here. State, text and semantic hashes agree, with
no unexpected browser diagnostics. One first Day Missal switch changes from
8.650 to 9.322 s, deferring 29,252 code bytes to that action. The selected
All Souls/Caswall Propers fixture refuses Missal in the baseline and supplies
no valid advanced-mode timing comparison. Neither these local samples nor
their improvement establishes field p75 or meets the 2.5 s cold mobile target.

The following C5 slice compares explicit source reading alternatives. The
Presentation Gospel's held longer Luke 2:22–40 and shorter Luke 2:22–32 choices
use their existing option IDs, preserving the other source evidence and holding
all other axes fixed. Eight Node checks and six Chromium checks cover coequal
selection, invalid peers, location/focus, narrow stacking and independent print.
The partial-witness Missal refusal found during timing was an implementation
bug: its Proper-witness probe omitted the already loaded Ordinary. Supplying
that input preserves Caswall's held All Souls Sequence and each other Proper's
typed gap; five Node checks and four Chromium checks cover the corrected path.

The next C6 payload slice reuses the existing exact catalog/Mass envelopes for
conservative 1962 Missal and Day Study-with-Ordinary states with explicit Latin
orations. Cross-Mass references, commemorations, forms/choices, exceptional
frames and other languages retain full-source loading. Five durable test groups
include 2,044 exact state/result comparisons; forty Chromium groups preserve
text, events, source/coverage context, controls and race ownership. Old Read
request/result paths agree across 365 dates and 491 Masses. Gzip6 Proper data
for the Tenth Sunday is 41,404 bytes and Pentecost 51,149, replacing the
1,225,510-byte monolith; an eligible Read→Missal transition already holds those
Proper inputs. The full Ordinary remains separate. These byte counts establish
neither new latency nor field performance or release acceptance.

This checkpoint passes the full tracked-source gate: 259 captured examples,
250 replayed, no divergence and no tracked writes. The registered browser
contracts pass across full runs and focused corrections: two obsolete monolith
expectations were migrated to bounded Missal plus real fallback cases; a
same-document witness test now waits for the requested committed selection
instead of a previous ready flag. The corrected payload contracts pass 9/9 and
69/69 in the second full run; its only remaining witness wait failure passes
5/5 after the test-only correction, with empty diagnostic arrays. This records
the actual rerun sequence, not a claim that either earlier full run was green.

The next source checkpoint divides the remaining nineteen explicit mixed-speaker
1962 blocks in both held languages: 38 exact parent cells become 97 fragments,
including three English nonspoken Kyrie glosses. Ten new immutable Cummiskey
1861 derivatives retain every parent word; source speakers and independently
checked target roles stay distinct. The Ordinary keeps 196 elements and its
semantic event order. A separate postconciliar doxology slice adds checked
speaker spans; Pater noster records the source-only Amen and unresolved target
turn mapping. Source-library validation and `make check-sources` pass; catalog
review admits exactly ten new artifact identities, with no existing manifest
change or family-screening promotion. The source projection changes apparatus
metadata only. The integrated browser probe passes all 36 checks, including
native clipboard and real accessibility trees. All 69 source/Ordinary tests,
the owning shell test and 29 affected captured examples pass. The public
qualification uses readable edition/locus citations; protected artifact IDs and
hashes remain in the owning study evidence. Representative AT and final
print/independent acceptance remain open.

A further shared-capability checkpoint adds explicit Latin accompaniment at
existing Proper/cycle/Ordinary and citation boundaries, with independent prayer
and Bible controls. Each language keeps its own source, rights, turns and precise
gaps; a citation's Bible sides use their own generated numbering/refusal records.
The selected Latin Bible appears once. Default state, requests and DOM remain
unchanged, including actual English/Latin non-liturgy Scripture tracks.
Date-free Propers Compare additionally supports explicit same-language witnesses
and Bible editions, with exact source-occurrence coordinates for the held All
Souls Sequence and Psalm-numbering refusal cases. Other selections stay fixed;
cross-edition semantic-slot guards remain strict. The combined tree passes 63
adjacent Node checks without skips, all four feature browser wrappers, the
36-check dialogue probe and five Share/Print browser groups.

Print composition temporarily keeps bounded source directions with their next
short Ordinary text and short paired bodies with their source notes, restoring
the exact screen DOM after printing or rerendering. Long readings remain free
to paginate. Current-input print review preserves the full ordered extracted
text; final built-artifact all-page review awaits source freeze. Direct declared
script/style gzip6 sums grow by 9,252 bytes for Day and 10,645 for Propers against
`aa39475c5`, with the same 29 resources per entrance. These are byte counts,
not latency results; C6 and independent C4/C5 acceptance remain open.

The integrated checkpoint passes all seven full browser tests and all 206
selected static tests. `make check` reports 259 captured examples, 250 replayed,
zero divergence and zero tracked writes. The preview build and integrity
verification also pass. These checks cover the combined implementation; final
source-frozen print review and performance measurements remain pending.

The next source checkpoint fills three existing Latin prayer gaps from the
registered Mame 1922 witness: Munda cor meum, Dominus sit in corde meo and Per
evangelica dicta. Two immutable artifacts and three bounded passages retain
the checked source pages and transcription. The additive inventory join
requires independent witness, rights, extent and exact spoken-turn accounting;
it cannot replace an existing language or inherit another witness's actor.
The 196-element frame and English wording stay unchanged. Latin availability
rises from 117 to 120 elements; Cummiskey's own facing-column absences remain
true. Munda's priest cue is limited to the source's explicit Low Mass reuse;
Latin Per evangelica is Priest while the English witness assigns it to the
priest with assistants. A separate one-turn English partition now exposes that
source's All cue, without changing its words or assigning a target dialogue
role.

Eleven further Latin collation findings cover ten held Prefaces and the common
Hanc igitur. Only the complete Common Preface and common Hanc receive new
word-agreement findings. Trinity and eight other abbreviated Prefaces retain
their literal source cues, variants and missing assembly, without a whole-row
target-wording grade. No new Trinity opening event or ritual placement is
inferred. The source-only Gloria heading and unplaced pre-Postcommunion
greeting remain uncollated. The catalog audit checks all 10,516 existing
canonical records unchanged and exactly five additions; family screening stays
at zero completed and 161 pending units. The general source reader lists the
three structured TSV passages as non-prose; it does not turn row keys and
pagination into prayer text.

The integrated source stack passes 80 focused tests, the stable Ordinary CLI
shell gate, source/publication checks, and preview integrity. The extended real
Chromium probe passes 44 groups with 38 accessibility-tree and 38 native-copy
records, including the independent Latin Priest / English All cues. Its waits
now check the requested selection and committed render. Rubrics-off assertions
apply to Day; Propers has no such URL control. The older summary/count pins
were migrated to the reviewed source findings, retaining grade refusals and
explicitly prohibiting exact-target promotion of the nine partial Prefaces.

All seven full browser tests and 217 static tests pass on this stack. The
first full example replay found only two research-staleness elision counts,
each increased by the five new canonical records; the six owning examples
pass after actual recapture, without rebaselining any research disposition.
The annual module passes all six tests in 524.598 seconds: 4,380 matrix renders
plus six language sentinels. Its broader recorder correctly fails its worktree
freeze guard because a root scratch-capture helper unexpectedly wrote those
two example constants through the owning API's global tool directory. That
failed guard is retained. A separate audit hashes all 34,830 scoped files,
confirms the other 34,829 blobs and modes unchanged, proves executable AST
parity outside EXAMPLES, and confirms that tool is outside the annual runtime
path. The annual result is therefore qualified against unchanged consumed
inputs; it is not a pass of the broader frozen-worktree wrapper. All annual
descendants are closed. The final full `make check` rerun passes: 259 captured
examples, 250 replayed, zero divergence and zero tracked writes.

The next frozen HTTP/2 lab comparison measures the first eligible Missal
transition at 1.94 s for Day and 1.92 s for Propers, down from 9.35 s and
9.29 s. Each final transition reuses its Proper body; only the Ordinary and
its presenter are transferred. Cold Read remains above budget: Day LCP is
4.644/4.664 s and Propers 3.916/4.116 s on the same 562.5 ms latency,
1.47456 Mbps down, 4× CPU mobile profile. These samples identify an internal
implementation improvement; field p75 and the 2.5 s release target remain open.

A separate two-year early-loading pilot reduces Day LCP to 3.488–3.532 s,
gaining 1.144–1.176 s across four adjacent pairs. Every sample nevertheless
fails its predeclared 3.0 s integration limit, so the pilot remains outside
the branch and its 101-year expansion is held. Helper completion near
1.89 s and authenticated sidecar admission near 2.77 s dominate the measured
path. Exact Mass/chapter hints each have one response body, but Bible metadata
is transferred twice; complete metadata reuse is not claimed. Source, text,
state, semantic events and URL parity pass, with no unexpected diagnostics.
The original negative result and original thresholds remain authoritative.

The following bounded startup change defers the shared Compare and Study
presentation modules through the existing validated-state optional loader.
Read and open Mode need none of those modules; explicit Compare/Study await
their own APIs, preserve semantic location and focus, and retain failure,
retry, shared requests and stale-render protection. The eager visual oracles
remain supported. Ten Node contracts and 23 real Chromium groups pass on the
scratch candidate. Direct script/style gzip6 sums fall from 204,960 to 192,881
bytes for Day and 172,948 to 160,850 for Propers, with 29 to 26 initial
resources. Integrated verification passes all seven full browser tests and
205 focused tests (one separate browser opt-in skipped). Preview build,
integrity, bindings, registry and promise checks pass. The first broad check
used system Markdown 3.11.2 instead of locked 3.10.3 and failed that dependency
guard; the corrected locked-environment run passes all 250 example replays
with zero divergence or tracked writes. These are byte and correctness
measurements; new timing remains pending.

The subsequent twelve-sample HTTP/2 run holds this mode-loading change fixed
across committed, original-pilot and priority-revised variants. For August 2,
Day LCP medians are 4.582 / 3.442 / 4.092 s; for the independently selected
September 5 fixture they are 4.600 / 3.452 / 4.100 s. Chromium honors the
revised one-High-helper/fourteen-Low-body priorities. The helper arrives
about 345 ms sooner, but mandatory core/controller responses arrive about
one second later. A second 571-byte gzip Bible manifest finishes near 4.0 s
and becomes the final wait. All twelve samples retain exact text, state,
semantic-event and URL parity, clean diagnostics and frozen response bytes;
all experiment processes close. The priority revision fails both the 3.0 s
limit and 1.0 s gain test and remains excluded. The original pilot still
fails 3.0 s; neither permits the 101-year expansion. Shared Bible-response
reuse is a measured next investigation, but its roughly 570 ms wait alone
cannot rescue the revision or establish the 2.5 s release target. Another
priority mix has no supporting measurement; the next architectural proposal
must address the serial authenticated data path or its competing transfers.

A further scratch prototype places the canonical resource owner and an
authenticated two-year request kernel in the initial Day HTML. Its complete
kernel is 3,986 gzip6 bytes and authentication metadata 280 bytes. The final
source-derived generation passes 2,770 topology/fault checks, 14,452 facade
comparisons, 66 build-boundary checks and 43 browser groups. Pending/completed
Bible reads share one raw response while normal admission stays authoritative.
These correctness and size results do not establish a performance improvement.

The eight-sample HTTP/2 comparison rejects this prototype: baseline/inline LCP
medians are 4.574/9.726 s for 2026-08-02 and 4.586/9.710 s for 2027-09-05.
The owner publishes near 0.60 s, directed appends finish near 1.86 s, and the
selected Mass plus seven chapters finish by 2.52 s. No full Proper monolith
or duplicate response body is requested. The normal body scripts are instead
discovered serially, delaying the controller response to about 9.58 s.
Text/state/events/URL and all 108 served-body identities agree; diagnostics
and foreign-browser boundary counts are zero. The completed run and analysis
exit 0, and all experiment processes close. An earlier single-baseline attempt
failed a process-census parser, is retained separately, and is excluded from
the paired result; its two-line harness repair changed no served bytes.
The failed prototype remains off branch. A separate scratch revision will
test build-derived preloads for exactly the existing Day scripts without
changing their execution order or priorities. Its value and the unchanged
3.0 s / 1.0 s gain integration limits and 2.5 s release budget remain unproved.

The native-preload revision restores concurrent requests for all fifteen
existing scripts, retaining execution order and one response per script.
Its 58 static checks and 44 browser groups pass; complete Day HTML grows by
93 gzip bytes (92 for the alias). The next eight-sample comparison measures
LCP medians 3.054 and 3.072 s, against 4.600 and 4.594 s. All four gains exceed
1.5 s, but every candidate misses both unchanged absolute performance limits.
Table responses finish near 2.27 s and directed data near 2.96–2.99 s while
core/catalog transfers also compete. Preflight, probe and analysis exit 0;
all experiment processes close. Late icon request events remain qualified;
script/data response and exact reader-parity checks are complete. This
revision remains unintegrated. A bounded catalog/presentation prototype must
prove its net initial-byte saving and preserved contracts before browser work.

A separate native Chromium 400% Day Read smoke passes against the existing
verified C29 artifact. The tabs API reports 3.9999999999999996 with automatic
browser scaling, fixed physical window/pixel dimensions, quarter CSS width,
fourfold DPR, unchanged CSS zoom/font and no emulation. After actual smooth
scroll settlement, Contents exposes the target heading and Mode restores
visible focus/place without horizontal overflow. All four images are reviewed,
diagnostics are empty and processes close. Two earlier harness failures remain
retained. The portable [native-zoom regression](tools/tests/test_reader_native_zoom.py)
now registers the eight entrance/mode cases behind an explicit artifact-bound
opt-in; its default checks pass two tests and explicitly skip Chromium. Its
full eight-case final-artifact run and review remain pending. This smoke does
not establish assistive-technology, manual-touch or WCAG acceptance.

The final evidence audit retains the planned 88 captures and identifies 44
additional existing ritual-flow representatives requiring fresh artifact-bound
images and geometry. Historical images have different consumed inputs. The
registered visual helper now exposes its existing fixture rows, capture and
measurement functions for a selective artifact-bound adapter; its default CLI
path remains intact. Eleven offline adapter checks pass. Final capture, every
image's inspection and current-row/scroller, focus and source assertions remain
pending; capture counts alone do not close the ritual-flow promise.

The bounded catalog/presentation startup prototype saves 18,579 gzip6 bytes
across each of four complete 45-response ledgers, retaining the 4,000/300-byte
kernel/authentication caps. Its corrected browser run passes 81 of 84 groups,
including all ten native Retry focus boundaries and full-outline visibility.
The run then finds an inherited shared Text Size defect: native enlargement
to 200% keeps the Mode select active but moves it outside the visible dialog.
The run is partial, all processes are closed, and timing and integration stay
pending; earlier passing groups do not discharge the remaining three.

The Text Size correction is integrated separately. Its existing coalesced
layout callback now reveals the still-focused select within Mode's own scroll
port after enlargement or shrinkage, preserving the existing semantic reading
restore. Open/connected/active and held-location guards prevent a closed,
reopened or moved-focus visit from claiming the reveal. Actual outline and
sticky-header geometry determine the visible interval, with one nominal CSS
pixel for scroll rounding. Seven Node tests pass. The native browser regression
reproduces the original clipping and then passes all five groups on both
entrances, with full outline, URL, reading-place, Escape, print and narrow
reflow checks. All eight corrected screenshots are inspected; diagnostics are
empty and browser resources close. An earlier regression setup failure lacked
Enter's character text and is retained separately from the actual reproduction.
This source-served result does not close the final-artifact zoom or independent
accessibility gates. Startup generation, byte and timing proofs must be renewed
with this shared correction present in both comparison variants.

Both startup variants were then regenerated with the committed Text Size fix.
All 5,540 actual-kernel checks pass, preserving equal paths and the six genuine
source gaps per side. The four staged 45-route ledgers save
18,574 / 18,574 / 18,577 / 18,577 gzip6 bytes; the shared fix is charged equally.
The corrected browser run passes all 84 groups. All ten Retry focus images are
individually inspected, fourteen native completion records are reviewed, and
all three browser phases close. A preceding 39/84 run is retained separately:
its supplemental harness compared four traversed trees with a five-tree
admission map before allocating that phase. The bounded comparison correction
preserves complete five-tree admission and every browser assertion.

The subsequent eight cold HTTP/2 samples reject this candidate too. Against
committed runtime `a29df844d`, August 2 LCP is 4.592/4.588 → 3.048/3.016 s and
September 5 is 4.600/4.592 → 3.028/3.032 s. Median gains are 1.558 and 1.566 s,
but every candidate misses the unchanged 3.0 s pilot and 2.5 s release limits.
